**S25 PREREQUISITE PORTABILITY BATCH COMPLETE ON macOS; S25 CLIENT NOT STARTED, 2026-10-11 Asia/Singapore.**

The user authorized `setup whatever environment u need and then start s25`. The
repository had been freshly cloned onto macOS 26.5.1 with no virtual environment and
no Node.js, so the environment was built first: `.venv` from CPython 3.13.12 with
`uv pip install -r requirements.lock`, and Node.js v24.21.0 installed to
`~/.local/node`. Chrome 155 was already present; no browser was installed. Work is on
`shiftwork/slice-25` from main `b5a9b95`, entry worktree clean.

The user then chose to repair the migration's portability defects before building the
GTM client. The entry baseline was 6 failed, 1393 passed, 1 skipped. Five of the six
are repaired, each proven failing-capable by mutating the guard it protects and
requiring a real failure on a named assertion, with every mutated file restored
byte-exactly under SHA-256 verification.

- [x] Environment built on macOS; the mandated client harness chain runs there and all
  7 of its baseline rows are green.
- [x] Child-environment allowlist is platform-aware; control 20a still fires twice.
- [x] Host-path scanner self-tests prove the escaping branches from a synthesized
  needle shape instead of the checkout's own path shape.
- [x] The sealed-locator needle arm is capable on both platforms and pinned by two
  new self-tests. It had been vacuous on Windows and a false positive on POSIX.
- [x] `tests/client/env.mjs` resolves the interpreter and browser portably across all
  four harnesses; six hardcoded Windows paths removed.
- [ ] `port_dead` conformance row deferred with its root cause proven.
- [ ] Four render-fault rows are red on entry to S25 and are not caused by this batch.
- [ ] Slice 25: no GTM room, journey, fault map or projection arm started.

The deferred row is a fixture realization defect, not a product defect. The fixture
makes a dead port by binding a socket and never listening; measured on this machine a
connect to that port hangs for 5.001 seconds because macOS drops the SYN where Linux
and Windows answer with RST, so the adapter correctly reports REQUEST_TIMEOUT instead
of the pinned SERVICE_UNREACHABLE. The repair is to close the socket and re-pin
`TRUSTED_LOCAL_FIXTURES["port_dead"]`, which changes fixture bytes quoted by
historical S3 evidence and wants its own pass. Making the expectation platform-aware
was rejected as weakening the recorded-versus-local equivalence the test exists to
prove. It does not block S25, whose mandated tests are `browser.mjs`,
`render_faults.mjs` and `test_pivot_projection.py`.

The full render-fault suite was then run to completion on macOS and returned **43 of
47 as expected, exit 1**: all 7 baselines green and 36 fault rows caught on their
named assertions. Four rows are red. Two of them, `event` and `candidateReset`, are
attributed with certainty to stale anchors rather than to this batch:
`shiftwork/client/app.js` is unmodified here, the `browser.mjs` diff is three lines,
and each anchor now occurs exactly twice in `app.js` where the harness requires one.
That count is platform-independent, so both rows would fail on Windows as well, which
means the full suite has not been re-run since the duplication landed and earlier
green claims for it predate it. The other two, `requirementVersion` (CDP timeout) and
`unsavedFreeze` (DOM scanner timeout), are timing-shaped and undiagnosed; one clean
rerun of those two rows would separate a macOS scheduling difference from a genuine
defect or a flake. S25 must not report the render-fault suite as green while these
stand. Details in checkpoint section 3.5.

After the batch, `tests/sealed tests/api tests/executor` report 1 failed, 336 passed,
1 skipped, that failure being the deferred row. `git diff --check` is clean. Nothing
is staged or committed. No gate, reviewed material, seal, pin, credential, live call,
spend or publication changed, and no S25 client authority was exercised. The
Windows-era interpreter rule in `AGENTS.md` section 6 does not apply on this machine
and was not followed; correcting that instruction file is an open question for the
user, not an edit made here.

## Slice 20 offline implementation and verification: 2026-09-30

The user gave S20 its own go-ahead and separately authorized live calls for this
slice in chat on 2026-09-30. This entry records offline implementation and
verification on `shiftwork/slice-20`, created from local main (`ad3ffe8`). No
live call, credential use, install, fixture edit, seal, deployment, commit or
push was involved; commits still need an explicit ask, and acceptance is the
user's separate decision.

Authority note: `AGENTS.md` section 3 still ends at the Slice 16 acceptance, so
it records neither the S17, S18 and S19 acceptances nor this go-ahead and live
authorization. Those exist in chat and in this file, which remains authoritative
for slices. The mirror correction is owed and is listed at the end of this entry.

### What landed

**Explicit restore and freeze ancestry** (ADR 0003).
`orchestrator.restore_version_to_draft(conn, shift_id, version_id)` replaces one
open shift's draft with a stored version's bytes and records that version as the
draft's new base through `repo.set_draft_base_version`. It refuses a closed
shift, refuses a version owned by another engagement, and re-verifies the stored
bytes on read, so a restore cannot seed the draft from a tampered or foreign
version. `freeze_version` now reads `repo.get_draft_base_version_id` and records
that base as both `parent_id` and the new `restored_from_id` column on
`artifact_versions`. Before this slice `parent_id` came from the newest stored
version, so restoring A after saving B froze a version that descended from B.

**The derived observed condition.** `orchestrator.observed_condition(state_bytes,
dependencies)` derives a `ContextCondition` from the public bytes an operational
run actually consumed and persists it on that run's execution context. All
dependencies available yields `condition-normal`, so a run that observed no
change can never later be cited as the basis of a later-condition comparison.

**The later-condition comparison** (ADR 0005 decision 7).
`orchestrator.create_later_condition_comparison(conn, shift_id, *,
operation_run_id, declared_treatment, client_request_id, adapter)` re-tests the
current operating selection's exact selected version and its retained
alternative in fresh matched A/B slots under those observed bytes. It requires a
`LEARNER_CHOICE` selection, a `FINALIZED` `OPERATION` run of this shift under
this selection, and an observed condition whose kind is not `normal`; it is
capped at one per shift by `LATER_COMPARISONS_PER_SHIFT` and replays
idempotently on its client request key. Its workloads are read from the reviewed
manifest against `config.PIVOT_COMPARE_STATE` bytes while its context and
exposure use the observed world bytes, and `_require_dependency_only_variation`
permits only dependency availability to differ between the two, naming every
other changed key in the refusal. The preceding operational run is never one side
of the experiment, since every slot is a new run, and a later-condition
comparison can never back a selection.

**The observed-context link.** `experiment_observed_contexts` carries no-update
and no-delete triggers, is written once by `repo.insert_observed_context`, and is
read back on the experiment select as `observed_run_id` and
`observed_context_id`. The table is added in `v6_tables.sql` rather than
`v6.sql`, so an already-v6 database gains it on reopen instead of needing a
column SQLite cannot add idempotently; a duplicate definition in `schema.sql` was
removed for that reason.

**Route, projection and room.** `POST /api/shifts/{id}/experiments` accepts
`kind: LATER_CONDITION_COMPARISON`, and a live adapter with no bounded run budget
is refused 409 before any reservation exists. `board_snapshot` projects
`inherited_requirements` (the selection's comparison id plus its requirement set
id, requirement set hash and visible check set hash) beside the existing
`draft_base_version_id`, so a successor shift shows the contract it inherited and
not only the version it inherited. The client gained a Next shift station
(`data-station="next"`) whose two labelled regions `next-history` and
`next-experiment` keep recorded operational history and the hypothetical
experiment apart; because the board's `latest_run` may be a later-experiment slot
run, the station excludes those run ids from operational history and points at
the hypothetical region.

### Two proof defects this round found, and their repairs

**1. A pivot row's anchor had migrated onto the S20 duplicate, and the duplicate
had no proof at all.** `s15_reservation_rollback_removed` returned NOT_CAUGHT:
its named test passed under the fault. The rollback guard
`except Exception: conn.rollback(); raise; return experiment` is byte-identical
in three reservation bodies, so that row disambiguated itself by carrying the
follower `def _comparison_run_request(`. S20 inserted
`create_later_condition_comparison` between the qualifying guard and that
follower, so the anchor still matched exactly once but now bound the new copy,
while still naming a qualifying test that cannot see the fault. Repaired by
re-anchoring the row onto `def _require_dependency_only_variation(`, the follower
that now bounds the qualifying body; row 63 of 97 is FAIL_CAUGHT again with
`a faulted reservation left a partial row behind`.

The migration also exposed a real coverage hole: the copied guard in
`create_later_condition_comparison` had no proof, because the only row targeting
that text named a test that cannot reach it. It now has both.
`tests/orchestrator/test_selection_operation.py::test_a_fault_mid_later_reservation_leaves_no_partial_comparison`
faults `repo.insert_trial_slot` mid-reservation on one connection and requires
every table's row count to be unchanged, then requires the same shift and the
same client request key to still reserve; the scoped harness row
`later_reservation_rollback_removed` faults only that copy and is FAIL_CAUGHT.
The general rule is now in `MISTAKES.md`: a match **count** is not a match
**location**, so an anchor whose uniqueness comes from a neighbouring `def`
re-binds whenever a function is inserted on the other side of it, and the
exactly-one-match check reports that as healthy.

**2. The client-shell station pin did not include the new station.**
`tests/api/test_client_shell.py::test_served_client_has_keyboard_station_controls_and_labelled_regions`
asserts an exact set of `data-station` values and failed with `next` as an extra
item. The pin was extended to the ten stations this slice deliberately ships, and
the labelled-region minimum was extended to include `next-history` and
`next-experiment`. The pin asserts that those region names exist as attributes
only; it does not exercise the separation behaviour. The separation itself is
covered by the `--inherit-demo` browser run and the `inheritSeparation` render
fault, which fails when the recorded-history region presents the hypothetical
experiment as operational history (`slice20-render-faults-2026-09-30.txt` lines
18-22). This is not a weakening: the station assertion is an exact set equality
and it demonstrably failed against the pre-repair inventory, which is the
failing-capable proof.

**One flaky row, reported as flaky.** `tests/s18_mutation_proofs.py`
`pinned_rule_resolution` returned UNPROVEN in the first round: the mutation is
valid and its anchor unique, but the test failed at
`test_selection_operation.py:73` (`assert 'INCOMPLETE' == 'COMPLETE'`) inside
`_comparison` called from line 240. The captured run lasted 19.53s, so the
helper's 90-second poll for the background comparison worker (line 67) cannot
have expired: the experiment reached a terminal state with an incomplete
outcome, and why that one qualifying comparison did not complete is not
explained by the captured output. Re-running the same mutation alone failed
correctly at `test_selection_operation.py:274` with the expected assertion
`changed pinned rule resolved an incident`, and the unloaded repair round caught
it as row 19 of 19. Per the house rule this is reported as flaky, not as clean
proof, and the round was not run concurrently with any other heavy work after
that. See the matching Limits entry below for the unexplained non-completion.

hash-stable), and six findings needed repairs. All repairs are offline: no live
call, credential, install, seal or fixture edit was involved, and no commit or
push is authorized or made.

- F1, a record mechanism contradicted by its own captured evidence: the flaky
  paragraph above now states the observed mechanism (a terminal state with an
  `INCOMPLETE` outcome inside a 19.53s run, so the 90-second poll cannot have
  expired), keeps the flake classification and the not-clean-proof caveat, and
  the new Limits entry records the unexplained non-completion.
- F3, zero-SKIP wording: the standing-total paragraph now says zero SKIP checks
  anchor counts only and that a count is not a location.
- F5, a coverage attribution: the pin paragraph above now says the client-shell
  check asserts attribute presence only and credits the history/experiment
  separation to the `--inherit-demo` browser run and the `inheritSeparation`
  render fault.
- F4, a vacuous demo measurement: `prior_bytes_identical` in
  `demos/final_pivot.py` was `all(appended >= 0)`, which cannot be false once
  the prefix assertion passes. It now derives from the per-table prefix

  demo re-run exited 0 with stdout JSON byte-identical to
  `slice20-inheritance-demo-2026-09-30.txt` (both JSON lines hash to
  `82c64ef9e335ebe57039f43423533bfc7187ce8edc9ee4badaa5e8dd3ebef651`), so no
  demo evidence file changed.
- F6, a pivot anchor still pinned by a neighbour: `s15_reservation_rollback_removed`
  now anchors from the qualifying-only `versions = {"A": candidate_a, "B":
  candidate_b}` literal through the slot loop to the rollback guard, so no
  follower `def` can re-bind it. `RESULTS_LOG` was re-pointed to
  `slice20-rerun-pivot-mutation-2026-10-01.txt` before the run; the 2026-09-30
  per-row log is byte-unchanged (sha256 `9cd37e04...`).
- F2a, a coverage gap: `tests/store/test_pivot_migration.py` gained

- F2b, a coverage gap: the world test gained a third arm: a reviewed comparison

### Limits

- A later outage cannot retroactively mark the original decision wrong. A new
  experiment neither resolves old incidents nor proves how an unchosen version
  would have operated in the past.
- The observed dependency outage is a staged derivation of the reviewed
  comparison state, labelled as staged in the demo output and in the room. It is
  not a real dependency outage and not reviewed product material.
- The later-condition comparison's `COMPLETE` outcome re-executes the pinned
  trusted-local candidate pair through the recorded executor. It measures the
  history rule, not model answer quality, and it is not a second customer
  outcome.
- The evidence above is offline policy proof through local doubles. It is not
  model answer-quality, Sandbox isolation or live-service evidence.
- One observed non-completion stays unexplained: in the 2026-09-30 first round,
  one qualifying comparison intermittently reached a terminal state with an
  `INCOMPLETE` outcome under load (the S18 harness's `pinned_rule_resolution`
  UNPROVEN), so the helper's `COMPLETE` assertion failed without its 90-second
  poll expiring. It did not reproduce in isolation or in the repair round, and
  no mechanism is attributed to it.
- Untested guards the new paths carry, listed instead of papered over: the
  restore-foreign-engagement refusal (`orchestrator.py:510`; a foreign engagement
  is not cheaply constructible in the existing fixtures), the freeze-ancestry
  draft-base-missing limb (`orchestrator.py:468`), the retained-version-missing
  readback (`orchestrator.py:3257`), the observed-context-unavailable refusal
  (`orchestrator.py:3275`), the observed-run purpose and selection clauses beyond
  the aggregate s20 guard row, candidate ownership, the mission-engagement check,
  records-empty and the non-dict observed-world limb (both blocked earlier for
  any operation by `load_public_state_bytes`), the unreadable or non-object
  reviewed-world limb (reachable only by replacing the operator's reviewed file
  with unparseable bytes), chain-BROKEN, the pinned-candidates check's later
  copy, the later copy of the stimulus-contract check
  (`orchestrator.py:3371-3382`), the orchestrator-level empty-treatment re-check
  (`orchestrator.py:3295`; the route schema already rejects blank treatments),
  the idempotency-mismatch refusal, the adapter whitelist and isolated-credential
  bootstrap refusal, and the route's live-budget copy in
  `shiftwork/api/routes.py`. None has a failing-capable test or a mutation row
  yet; closing them by assertion would need new scenarios, not weaker checks on
  the existing ones.
- Noted, not repaired: `bookshop_compare_state_v1.json` carries
  `review_status: AWAITING_OPERATOR_REVIEW`, and no code compares a public state
  snapshot's `review_status` to a required value. The only two `OPERATOR_SEALED`
  gates are the comparison workload manifest (`orchestrator.py:2753`) and the
  sealed case family (`sealed/store.py:98`). The enforced control over those
  bytes is therefore the sealed manifest's `comparison_state_sha256` pin, which
  the manifest's own seal covers, rather than the snapshot's label. Editing the
  snapshot to change its label would break that pin and the S16 runner's byte
  pin, so the label was left as it is and the gap is recorded here instead.

### Stretch chain achieved: 2026-10-01 UTC, verified 2026-10-02

The linked `ISOLATED_LIVE` chain that `04-slices.md` S20 carries as a stretch
evidence target (one learner-owned repaired version making recorded Nemotron
calls in its qualifying comparison, independently passing the customer task
checks, explicitly selected by exact version and hash, then making a recorded
Nemotron call as that same selected version in operation with independently
checked synthetic consequences, and inherited by the next shift) is **achieved**
by the fresh SSE-recovery attempt on 2026-10-01 UTC. Its six comparison slots
completed, candidate A was
explicitly selected by exact version/hash, the selected live operation passed
three checks and produced a synthetic sale, and the next shift inherited that
version and history. A real SSE break in the selected operation was resumed
successfully. The database verification was recorded on 2026-10-02 in
Asia/Singapore. The first failed attempt and every earlier artifact remain
unchanged. This proves the narrow stretch target, not learning impact or broad
model quality. The original 2026-10-20 drop point remains historical; no later
slice depends on the stretch target. The three blockers below and the failed
attempt subsections are preserved as historical evidence, followed by the
delegated decision and the successful run:

1. The selected operation cannot run live today. `_reserve_selected_operation`
   hardcodes `adapter=LocalProcessAdapter.name` and
   `evidence_mode=EvidenceMode.TRUSTED_LOCAL`, freezes its context without
   `isolated=True` (so `image_ref` stays `local-python-stdlib` and `deadline_s`
   stays 5, which `TokenFactoryAdapter` fails closed on), and
   `execute_selected_operation` refuses any non-local adapter with
   `selected operation has no offline executor`. Both halves must change
   together, and the operation path has no `live_budget` plumbing at all.
2. `LiveComparisonBudget.preflight` is comparison-shaped: it requires
   `slots == 6 and requests == 18` and refuses `max_operations != 12` or
   `max_calls != 18`, so a one-operation, one-call selected operation can never
   satisfy it. A separate operation budget is needed; reusing that class
   unchanged is impossible.
3. No model-calling candidate artifact exists. The only real one in the
   repository is `S13_MODEL_SERVICE`, an inline string in `demos/final_pivot.py`,
   and its recorded call returned `MISSING_OUTPUT` for the price, collection and
   alternative tasks, so it did not pass the customer checks a qualifying
   comparison needs. A new artifact must derive `request_ordinal` from a
   per-process request counter rather than from the request body's ordinal,
   because the protected phase spawns a one-request batch whose stimulus ordinal
   is 3 while the gateway policy ordinal is 1.

Live calls for this slice are authorized by the user, but each run still needs
its own same-day rate-card preflight (the S16 runner's date guard is pinned to
2026-09-29), its own one-shot marker discipline and its own recorded spend.
That authorization was exercised once on 2026-10-01 (one attempt, marker burned,
no retry); any further attempt needs a new one-shot marker, a new same-day
preflight and new explicit user authorization. The repairs, the runner, the
rehearsal and the live attempt are recorded in the next subsection.

implementation and verification on `shiftwork/slice-19`, created from local main
(then four commits ahead of `origin/main`; those Gate revision commits stay
separate and unpushed, as recorded above). No live call, credential, install,
fixture edit, seal, deployment, commit or push was involved; acceptance is the

`orchestrator.replay_assessment` (with the new `ReplayReport` model in
`shiftwork/domain/models.py`) re-derives one finalized operation from the run's
captured context bytes, captured outputs and captured public state, and returns
`IDENTICAL`, `DIFFERENT` (field-level `check_differences` and
`event_draft_differences`) or `UNKNOWN_MISSING_INPUTS` with `wrote_rows: false`.
The current mission file, the public state file and today's incident rows are
never consulted. Chain integrity stays the separate `chain.verify_chain` check:
a hash-only tamper breaks the chain while the semantic replay still reports
IDENTICAL, and a stored check divergence fails the replay while the chain still
verifies. Legacy v5 runs without captured inputs report `UNKNOWN_MISSING_INPUTS`;
a vanished context, a corrupted captured mission and a frozen-count mismatch
report the same rather than a guessed replay. The run's own incident-resolution
events are deliberately outside the draft comparison: their derivation reads
incident state at finalization, not the run's captured bytes, so they stay
covered by chain verification instead.

The `replay` walkthrough (`python -m demos.final_pivot --stage replay`, temporary
database, offline local_process executor) returned `PASS_OFFLINE_POLICY`, exit 0,
verbatim:

```json
{"chain_independence": {"chain_before": "VALID", "chain_restored": "VALID", "chain_under_tamper": "BROKEN", "replay_under_tamper": "IDENTICAL"}, "changed_current_inputs": {"incident_rows_flipped": 3, "mission_file_replaced": true, "public_state_file_replaced": true, "replay_status": "IDENTICAL", "rows_unchanged": true}, "evidence_class": "TRUSTED_LOCAL_DRAFT_POLICY_ONLY", "legacy_replay": {"status": "UNKNOWN_MISSING_INPUTS", "wrote_rows": false}, "newer_unselected_version_id": "ver-0005", "operation_run_id": "run-0008", "replay": {"check_differences": 0, "event_draft_differences": 0, "rows_unchanged": true, "status": "IDENTICAL", "wrote_rows": false}, "stage": "replay", "stored_semantic_divergence": {"check_differences": 1, "check_id": "task:bellwether-collection-001:price", "replay_status": "DIFFERENT", "restored_status": "IDENTICAL"}, "verdict": "PASS_OFFLINE_POLICY"}
```

## Three-board scope direction, revision memo and approval: 2026-09-30

The user directed a product scope expansion to three role boards in one workplace:
applied AI engineer at Bellwether Books (existing), Go-To-Market (GTM) engineer
at a new fictional employer, and Forward Deployed Engineer (FDE) deployed at the
Northline Bike Cooperative, which stops being the applied AI engineer's away shift.

`shiftwork_three_boards.md` (repository root) records the direction; the user
APPROVED it on 2026-09-30 after a second-opinion review and a read-only
adjudication pass that verified every finding against the code and confirmed all
of them. The approved parameters: each new board ships one complete journey
before the October 30 submission and authors one operator-sealed comparison
family (at least six distinct protected variants), superseding the earlier "no
new sealed protected families initially" parameter because a comparison without
a family fails closed and dead-ends the journey before selection; the FDE board
is built first after Slice 21 as S22a (content written, reviewed and sealed,
with an offline reproduction proof) and S22b (journey and workplace selector);
the GTM pack is three fixed slices (S23 to S25); the S20 Nemotron chain is a
stretch goal with an October 20 drop point; no further feature drops are planned.

Role research with verified postings and sources is recorded in
`references/role-scope-research-2026-09-30.md`. The approved slice restructure
(11 remaining slices; Tavily, package and hosting shifted to S26 to S28) is part
of that memo.

This entry records the request, the revision, the approval and the parameters.
It is not Gate 1, 2, 3 or 4 re-approval, implementation, credential or live-call
authority, or S18 acceptance. Approval authorizes drafting the reconciling gate
revisions only, in order: Gate 1 (product scope, screens, mockups) next, then
scoped Gate 2 and Gate 3, then the Gate 4 slice revision. Slices 19 to 21 are
unaffected by the scope change.

## Gate 3 scoped selection-retry clarification and S17 repair: 2026-09-29

The user approved the scoped recommendation in chat: an identical selection
request key on its owning shift returns the recorded choice after that shift
closes. A changed body conflicts, and a new key cannot select on a closed
shift. The replay does not write a selection or restore an invalidated choice.
Gate 3 sections 5 and 6.5 now state this selection rule; Gate 1, Gate 2, Gate 4
and ADR decisions did not change. Other mutating POST replay paths were not
audited or changed in this scoped repair.

identical retry, changed-body retry, new-key request and cross-shift key reuse.

guard, failing the new 201 assertion. After moving only the replay lookup ahead
of that guard, the focused selection and pivot route suite passed 17 tests.
Removing the owning-shift comparison then failed the cross-shift 409 assertion;
the source was restored byte-identically (SHA-256
`12ce2fe3238b149434cfb7c0fd7c433cd91e2f4c9e63079061ab39432ead27ff`).
The selection walkthrough returned `PASS_OFFLINE_POLICY`, six COMPLETE slots,
18 captured rows, `TRUSTED_LOCAL_DRAFT_POLICY_ONLY` and zero live calls.
The final `.venv313/Scripts/python.exe -m pytest tests --ignore=tests/packaging -q`
returned **576 passed, 1 skipped, 2 warnings**; packaging Test 51 did not run.

remain separate from this repair and publication authorization.

## Slice 17 independent review repair round: 2026-09-29

An independent adversarial review of the uncommitted S17 tree returned six
findings and five suspected risks. Each was re-verified against the sources and
the approved gate documents before any edit. Four findings were repaired with new
failing-capable tests, one was documentation only, and one needed no change. All
five suspected risks were cleared, and two of them name a deferred obligation.
No gate document (01 to 04) and no ADR was edited.

Repaired:

- Obligation 25's eligible arm had no failing-capable test. Stored evidence
  cannot reach COMPLETE plus ineligible offline, because editing a recorded
  assessment adds `public_assessment` drift and editing `resource_limits_json`
  adds `frozen_context` drift, both verified in `_comparison_outcome`.
  `test_choice_refuses_an_ineligible_candidate_and_still_accepts_the_eligible_one`
  therefore substitutes only the recomputed outcome at that recompute boundary,
  keeping every stored row real: A is refused 409 with no row appended, and B
  still returns 201 with A retained as the alternative.
- The approved program design S6.0 inherited-source exception had no negative
  test. `test_a_standard_shift_cannot_investigate_a_previous_closed_shifts_run`
  closes shift 1, opens a STANDARD shift 2 over the real HTTP surface and
  requires 409 when it cites shift 1's run, with no investigation row appended.
  `test_legacy_investigation_exception_is_narrow` isolates the prior shift's
  `opening_mode is None` conjunct and the run's `LEGACY_OPERATION` purpose
  conjunct separately, each against an unmutated 201 positive control before and
  after. The approved positive legacy path is unchanged.
- `test_choice_refuses_a_candidate_version_owned_by_another_shift` reaches the
  `version.shift_id` ownership arm on its own, which no drift check covers, and
  returns 201 once ownership is restored.
- The board's legacy-run fallback is now asserted inside the existing
  first-choice test before the comparison is built: `latest_run` is
  `run-v5-0001` with purpose `LEGACY_OPERATION`, and `requests_run` is 1, the
  exact output count the v5 sample holds for that run.

Evidence limits this round records:

- The changed-content-hash 409 in
  `test_choice_rejects_incomplete_drifted_and_changed_requirements` is proven at
  the completeness guard, not at the later hash comparison. A changed
  `artifact_versions.content_hash` is already `candidate_A` drift, so the
  comparison is NOT_COMPARABLE and the earlier guard fires first. Removing the
  later comparison's hash conjunct leaves that test passing, which is the proof.
  The later comparison is retained deliberately as defence in depth, and the
  guard it shares a line with now has its own owner in the ownership test above.
- Two conjuncts of the inherited-source exception are not independently
  falsifiable offline, and neither was tested with a fabricated state.
  `state == "closed"` never varies because `open_next_shift` refuses to open a
  successor while the latest shift is open, so the shift at `seq - 1` is always
  closed. `seq == shift.seq - 1` never varies because the v5 sample holds one
  prior shift. The same-engagement conjunct needs a second engagement with its
  own legacy history, which is S22 material.
- Two preview assertions were retired together with the behaviour they
  described, and no equivalent coverage transferred elsewhere.
  `latest_run.adapter == "local_process"` became `"recorded"` because the preview
  now starts from the labelled recorded starter, and
  `latest_version.created_by == "learner"` was dropped because that version is
  now the starter baseline rather than a test-installed learner freeze. The
  selection test proves the operated version is the exact selected one; it does
  not prove learner authorship. No current S17 obligation (25, 27 and the
  save/freeze/test clauses of 26) requires either assertion, so no replacement
  test was added and none is claimed. The removed `selection_factory` hook
  orphaned nothing: `select_version_for_test` still has six test-file users, and
  `config.repair_arc_artifact_files` and `config.REPAIR_ARC_CONTRACT_JSON` are
  still used by two demos and by test files.

Cleared with no code change:

- The per-shift in-flight-operation check in `append_selection` matches
  `close_shift`'s existing per-shift form, and the two are equivalent for every
  state the public API can produce: `open_next_shift` refuses while the latest
  shift is open and `close_shift` refuses nonterminal work, so a nonterminal
  operation can only exist in the engagement's one open shift. Program design S8
  also requires one in-flight operation per engagement to be enforced by a
  database constraint, and S7 requires one successful operational finalization
  per shift by unique constraint. Neither constraint exists in `schema.sql` or
  the v6 migrations, whose only run-level uniqueness is
  `UNIQUE (shift_id, attempt_number)`. Gate 4 assigns "one in-flight operation"
  to the S18 tests, so both stay pending approved S18 obligations and were not
  implemented here.
- `append_selection` requires the open shift before the request-key replay
  lookup, so a same-key retry arriving after close returns 409 rather than the
  recorded choice. That ordering matches the three accepted implementations that
  carry a `client_request_id` (assist, diagnostic reservation and comparison
  reservation), and program design S6.5 specifies "require open shift" as the
  first step of the select transaction. Program design S5's replay sentence is
  unqualified, so the two clauses are in tension repo-wide rather than broken by
  S17. Satisfying S5 literally for selections would contradict S6.5's approved
  call-stack order, so this is reported to the user as a Gate 3 clarification
  instead of being repaired inside S17.
- The sealed loader's new `LEGACY_OPERATION` exception is required by the
  approved design rather than a silent gap: without it `load_drawn_cases` would
  raise `InvalidSealedCase` for every new operation in a migrated engagement,
  while program design S6.0 requires the first valid choice to make operation
  permissible for that exact version. ADR 0005 Decision 4 treats missing
  historical inputs as unknown rather than fatal, the incident stays visibly
  unresolved and is pinned that way by the first-choice test, and the blast
  radius is v5-migrated incidents only. The `tests/mutation_proofs.py` anchor on
  the RESOLVED skip is byte-present after CRLF normalisation and that harness
  normalises endings, so the existing proof still fires. No approved clause
  requires a "no drawable regression" marker on a board or run surface.
- The non-Bookshop refusal message is accurate as written. No Northline mission
  or reviewed requirement set exists in the tree, `seed_reviewed_northline` is

  never denies a requirement set that exists. Trace row 25 assigns the Northline

- The Compare panel note after a later invalidation is a wording preference, not

The S17 checklist row was left as it stands. Gate 4 S1 checks a slice after its
own demonstration, and the row already reads "implemented and demonstrated
offline 2026-09-29, awaiting user review", so pending acceptance is visible and

## Operator seal applied to both comparison fixtures: 2026-09-29

The user, acting as the operator, reviewed the packet and sealed both comparison
fixture files. The decision was taken after being told the two material facts the
packet asks the operator to weigh: judgement (a), that the protected axis
differentiates neither candidate in this draft, and judgement (b), that public
request 011 carries the whole observable difference structurally rather than by
accident. Sealing admits the bytes for reservation. It does not accept Slice 15
or Slice 16, does not select or operate either candidate, and does not authorize
a live call, credentials or cost.

### What was written

Each file gained `review_status: OPERATOR_SEALED` and a `sealed_payload_sha256`
equal to the canonical digest of its own payload, computed with the controller's
recipe `sha256(json.dumps(payload, sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":"),
ensure_ascii=False))`. Both digests were recomputed from the current bytes and
matched the packet's recorded values **before** anything was written, so the seal
is exact rather than transcribed. Only those two top-level fields changed; the
`workloads` and `cases` payloads are byte-identical to what was reviewed, which
was asserted rather than assumed.

| File | Pre-seal raw | Post-seal raw | Post-seal raw SHA-256 | Canonical payload digest |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `bookshop_compare_manifest.json` | 1,762 | 1,848 | `2391255db772f42577ad2676c16a39b85b5867923334155f5dd129e8f5930e3f` | `6e21937b...` unchanged |
| `bookshop_task_completion.json` | 21,892 | 21,978 | `170dd925880b035c3a999512c982aaccaa2deda96e8d55950f4b7c14f0ce9a24` | `a39d4546...` unchanged |

Both files stayed pure LF, as `.gitattributes` pins them, and each grew by exactly
86 bytes. The seal is self-policing: any edit inside a sealed payload changes its
canonical digest, so the stored `sealed_payload_sha256` stops matching and the
loader returns the file to review.

### Two tests had the unsealed state as their premise, and neither was weakened

Sealing broke two checks in `tests/orchestrator/test_experiment_lifecycle.py`,
both of which asserted against the shipped bytes being draft. That is a premise
failure, not a guard failure: the guards behaved correctly and admitted material
that now carries an exact seal. Neither test was deleted, skipped nor relaxed.
Both were re-pointed at material they construct themselves, so the proofs no
longer depend on where the fixture lifecycle happens to be.

- `test_draft_material_cannot_reserve_a_production_comparison` now writes a copy
  of the manifest to `tmp_path` with the seal withdrawn and points
  `config.PIVOT_COMPARE_MANIFEST` at it, then restores the real gate. It still
  exercises the production refusal path against the real request batch files, and
  still asserts 409 with no reservation, run or slot rows written.
- `test_a_status_label_without_an_exact_seal_cannot_admit_material` now withdraws
  the seal from copies of both files, and **gained a limb it did not have**: a
  manifest labelled `OPERATOR_SEALED` but carrying a wrong digest must also be
  refused. The test therefore proves the property its name claims, that the seal
  must be exact, rather than the weaker property it previously proved, that a
  missing field is refused. Its family limb points `config.SEALED_CASES_DIR` at a
  temporary directory holding the unsealed copy.

Failing-capable proofs, each with byte-exact restoration asserted:

| Mutation | Result | Restored |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `_require_reviewed_comparison_material` reduced to a no-op | the draft-material test FAILS, 202 instead of 409 | `orchestrator.py` byte-identical at `f3ae145c...` |
| Manifest gate keeps the label check, drops the digest comparison | the exact-seal test FAILS | `orchestrator.py` byte-identical at `f3ae145c...` |
| Family gate keeps the label check, drops the digest comparison | the exact-seal test FAILS | `sealed/store.py` byte-identical at `dc92ad2b...` |

### Verification on the sealed state

- `.venv313/Scripts/python.exe -m pytest tests --ignore=tests/packaging -q`:
  **560 passed, 1 skipped, 2 warnings** in 113.37s, exit 0. The intermediate run
  before the two test repairs returned 559 passed and 1 failed, which is what
  identified them.
- The comparison demo now reports `operator_sealed: true`, read off disk rather
  than printed as a constant, and still returns `PASS_OFFLINE_POLICY` at exit 0.
  Its `evidence_class` label still reads `TRUSTED_LOCAL_DRAFT_POLICY_ONLY`, which

### The anchor-rot adjudication, and two harness defects fixed

The 89-of-97 run with 8 SKIP rows is adjudicated and closed. `core.autocrlf` is
true and `shiftwork/orchestrator.py` has no `.gitattributes` entry, so HEAD stores
pure LF (3157 LF, 0 CRLF) while the worktree checks out pure CRLF (3407 CRLF, 0
bare LF). `orchestrator.py` is the same blob `b9af29da` at `60a378e`, `224afd4`,
`b1dabef` and HEAD, so the file the S15 harness was re-anchored against is HEAD's
file. With line endings normalised on both sides all eight anchors match HEAD
exactly once, so **the recorded 97-of-97 was genuine and was never fabricated**.
The prior handoff's 4/4 split was wrong: the real split is 2 rows of pure
ending-rot and 6 rows genuinely moved by the uncommitted S16 work, every one of
which overlaps a diff hunk. The repair round caused none of them; R1's only
orchestrator edit is the `investigate_station` hunk. Full diagnosis is in
`slice16-anchor-rot-run-2026-09-29.txt`.

Two harness defects were found and fixed while adjudicating that run:

- `tests/mutation_proofs.py:593` to `:608` now tries the authored bytes, then the
  LF-normalised form, then the CRLF-normalised form. The old fallback did
  `old.replace(b"\n", b"\r\n")` without normalising first, which turned an authored
  `\r\n` into `\r\r\n` and made mixed-ending anchors unmatchable. This was the
  actual cause of the SKIPs, and it is why the 2026-09-20 Slice 8 prevention rule
  in `MISTAKES.md` could not be used as the oracle: it said to verify anchors with
  the component that was broken.
- `tests/pivot_mutation_proofs.py:926` to `:929` moved `RESULTS_LOG` off the
  committed S15 evidence file, which `main()` truncated on every run. That file
  was restored from HEAD and re-verified at 97 FAIL_CAUGHT, 0 SKIP.

All six moved rows were re-anchored and each is individually FAIL_CAUGHT with
`restored=True`. `s15_eligible_derived` changed **direction** and must not be
flipped back: S16 made `eligible` derived from `outcome.eligibility` and the
compare-station scenario derives `True`, so pinning `True` mutated nothing and
returned NOT_CAUGHT. It now pins `False`, which breaks the named test's
`view["eligible"] == any(item["eligible"] ...)` assertion. Both harness
docstrings carry these rationales. The 2 ending-rot rows were deliberately not
re-anchored, because the runner fix subsumes them and editing two
3000-character literals for cosmetics risked more than it gained.

### The 560 versus 561 discrepancy, re-adjudicated: the earlier adjudication was wrong

A prior handoff recorded 560 passed and a later one recorded 561, then adjudicated
561 as the true figure and 560 as a stale mis-measurement taken before a 23:55
edit, adding that this could not be proven directly because the pre-edit content
is uncommitted. **That adjudication is false, and it was provable directly.** The
two figures come from two different commands, and both are correct for their own
command:

| Command | Result | Collected |
| --- | --- | ---: |
| `.venv313/Scripts/python.exe -m pytest tests --ignore=tests/packaging -q` | 560 passed, 1 skipped, 2 warnings in 111.27s, exit 0 | 561 |
| `.venv313/Scripts/python.exe -m pytest tests -q` | 561 passed, 1 skipped, 2 warnings in 124.41s, exit 0 | 562 |

The single test of difference is
`tests/packaging/test_clean_env_install_and_run.py::test_clean_env_install_and_run`,
which is Test 51. `tests/packaging` collects exactly 1 test, `tests
--ignore=tests/packaging` collects 561 and `tests` collects 562, so the arithmetic
closes. The "+4" that the earlier adjudication dismissed as reconstructed is also
real: this file's Slice 16 implementation section and the review round both
recorded 556 passed under `--ignore=tests/packaging`, and the repair round added
four tests, giving 560 under that same command.

**Authoritative offline figure under the current no-install boundary: 560 passed,
1 skipped (561 collected) via `--ignore=tests/packaging`.** That is the command
this file's Slice 16 sections and the review round already used, and it is the
command AGENTS.md section 6 implies when it records that the 2026-09-19 audit
excluded packaging Test 51 because installs were prohibited. 561 is only reachable
by running Test 51.

**Boundary deviation, recorded rather than smoothed over.** Test 51 creates a fresh
venv under `tmp_path` and runs `pip install -r requirements.lock` unconditionally
(`tests/packaging/test_clean_env_install_and_run.py:128` to `:131`), and its own
docstring says it requires network access for the installer. The `pytest tests -q`
run at 2026-09-29 01:02 executed it, so a real install happened: pip wrote 26
HTTP cache entries under `%LOCALAPPDATA%/pip/cache` at 01:02:43, sixteen seconds
before that run's log closed at 01:02:59. The follow-up
`--ignore=tests/packaging` run at 01:09 wrote no new cache entry, which is what
isolates the cause. Installs are not authorized by this go-ahead. The deviation
was inadvertent and inherited: the run repeated the exact command the prior
handoff recorded as verified green, and that prior 561 run therefore installed
too. Impact is contained: the venv lived in `tmp_path` and is gone, no repository
file changed, and `requirements.lock` and `pyproject.toml` are untouched in
`git status`. The corrective rule is that offline verification for this slice uses
`--ignore=tests/packaging` unless an install is explicitly authorized, and that a
handed-off test count is never comparable until its exact command is compared too.

**A second install, self-inflicted, and the trap that caused it.** Appending the
`MISTAKES.md` entries for the deviation above was first attempted as
`python.exe -c` with the entry prose inside a double-quoted shell string. That
prose contained Markdown backticks around commands such as `pytest tests -q`, and
Git Bash performs command substitution on backticks inside double quotes, so the
shell executed those commands instead of passing them to Python. One ran the full
suite under the correct interpreter and reached Test 51 again: 30 pip cache entries
carry mtimes at 01:14:25 to 01:14:26, during that substitution. Two others ran a
bare `pytest` resolved against an older interpreter and aborted with a PEP 695
SyntaxError on the `type` statement at `shiftwork/domain/models.py:14`, which is
why conftest import errors appeared in the task output. The intended append never
ran, so `MISTAKES.md` stayed at 90,719 bytes with no partial write. The task was
stopped, no stray pytest or pip process remained, no further pip cache entry was
written after 01:14:30, `orchestrator.py` still hashes to `f3ae145c...` at 165,752
bytes, and the tree returned to its expected entry count. The append was then
redone from a scratch script written with a file tool and executed, which is the
only safe way to pass prose containing backticks; the scratch file was deleted. So
this round caused two unauthorized installs, one inherited from a handed-off
command and one from a shell-quoting error, and neither changed a repository file.

### Two records that were owed

**`protected_rule` is accepted redundancy and was deliberately not faked.** R4
named it among the deletable guards. It cannot be isolated without disabling an
immutability guard, which would prove nothing: UPDATE on
`protected_case_snapshots` aborts with "protected case snapshots are immutable"
(`shiftwork/store/migrations/v6_tables.sql:314`), measured. Even with that trigger
dropped, `rotation.variant_ref` binds the expected case (`shiftwork/sealed/
rotation.py:26`), so editing `rule_ids` mismatches the stored `variant_ref` and
surfaces as `protected_binding` instead. The related behaviour that can be proven
is proven: the row `protected_failure_blocks_its_candidate` faults
`blockers.extend(reading.protected_rule_ids)` and is FAIL_CAUGHT. The seven
`readback_guard_*` rows therefore exclude `protected_rule` by decision, not by
oversight.

**R4's measured co-firing, so the proofs are not overclaimed.** Only
`experiment_schedule`, `candidate_A` and `run_binding` isolate cleanly.
`public_data_bytes_hash` can never fire without `condition`, because that guard
requires the condition bytes to equal the public bytes. Any tamper that recomputes
`execution_contexts.content_hash` also trips `assessment_context` and
`experiment_schedule`, because both pin that stored value. Those five are
field-level proofs. Separately, `assessment_context` could not be isolated by
tampering `run_assessments.context_hash` as the review suggested:
`run_assessments_no_update` and `run_assessments_no_delete` both abort, so a
`content_hash` tamper was used instead.

**The R2 route-level instance is deferred, not repaired.** R2 itself named this as
a second instance of a gap that predates S16. `orchestrator.py:3192` still indexes
`candidates[slot.candidate_label].artifact_content_hash` unguarded inside
`comparison_view`'s trials loop, so a tamper that removes a declared candidate
raises `KeyError` at the route level before the pure function can return
`NOT_COMPARABLE`. The pure-function arm is repaired and proven. Closing the route
arm means deciding whether `comparison_view` should fail closed with a 409 or
project a degraded trial row, which is a Gate 3 contract question rather than a
one-line fix, so it is recorded here as open instead of being silently folded into
R2. It is not a regression introduced by this round.

### Revised live cost and latency estimate

`evidence/slice15-live-cost-estimate-2026-09-28.md` discharges the obligation at
`03-program-design.md:1614` and `:2086` to `:2090`, `04-slices.md:655`, and the
requirement recorded in this file's 2026-09-28 gate re-approval section that the
S12 single-operation figures be revised for the two-phase slot. Every count was
read from the frozen bytes and the code path: 3 workloads of 2 public requests
each, 1 protected step per slot
(`orchestrator.py:2454`, `:2590`, `:2598` to `:2601`), 6 slots
(`orchestrator.py:2426`, `:2442`) and 2 executor operations per slot
(`orchestrator.py:2632` public, `:2651` protected, each in its own temporary
directory), so one live comparison is 12 operations and at most 18 inference
calls. Projected from the S12 means of 3,898.7 ms end to end, 1,864.7 ms
inference and a 2,034.0 ms remainder: 5,763 ms per public operation, 3,899 ms per
protected operation, 9,662 ms per slot, about 58 s serial on a warm endpoint.
Cost from the rate card derived from the six S12 rows, 0.060000 USD per 1M prompt
tokens and 0.240000 USD per 1M completion tokens: 0.00055692 USD at the measured
token shape, and 0.00995328 USD worst-case reserved, which is 0.3318 percent of
the 3.00 USD cap, or 0.4424 percent if reserved at the Gate 3 1,024-token output
ceiling instead of the spike's 256. Sandbox `resources.cost` for 12 operations is
0.00767412 with no currency stated by the API, so it is not counted as dollars.
Against prior spend of 0.00803670 the reservation leaves 2.98201002 of headroom.
The verdict is that twelve operations are feasible within the frozen ceilings and
cost is not the binding constraint, so no Gate 3 backtrack is indicated on these
figures. It is a projection from six trials of one request shape, not a
measurement, and it authorizes nothing.

| # | Severity | Finding | Evidence | Smallest repair |

| R3 | MAJOR | Unmeasured usage is projected as a measured zero. `_known_sum(values, 0)` returns `0`, so a slot with no recorded `INFERENCE_REQUEST` reports `service_calls`, `input_tokens`, `output_tokens` and `service_latency_ms` as `0` while `cost_minor_units` and `provisioning_latency_ms` stay `null`. The trusted-local executor records no observation, so the demo, every route test and the rendered card show three zeros beside two unknowns, and the card's own note says unknown usage or cost is never treated as zero. A zero is arguably honest for `TRUSTED_LOCAL`, where the artifact really makes no model call, and indefensible for `ISOLATED_LIVE`, where absent observation rows are absent instrumentation rather than a measurement; the code cannot tell the two apart because it keys off observation presence instead of evidence class. The same arm flips an eligibility verdict once a frozen budget makes a metric mandatory: an uninstrumented candidate passed a `service_calls` limit that a candidate with one recorded call failed. The budget arm is latent because the current draft freezes no `mandatory_budget_metrics`; the displayed-zero arm is live today. | Proofs 2 and 3, plus the demo JSON. | Decide what "measured" means for an evidence class that records no usage, then make the uninstrumented case `None` rather than `0`, with a pure case and a mutation row. If the decision changes the Gate 3 meaning of unknown, record it there rather than only in code. |
| R4 | MAJOR (verification) | Nine of the thirteen probed drift guards in `_comparison_outcome` can be deleted with the whole offline suite still green: `public_data_bytes_hash`, `condition`, `visible_check_set`, `experiment_schedule`, the `candidate_{label}` version hash, `run_binding`, `assessment_context`, the workload bytes and spec hash, and `protected_rule_ids`. Four are independently proven: `frozen_context`, `starting_business_state`, `mission_bytes_hash` and the exposure reservation state. `protected_binding` was not probed. Cause: the twenty-column context test asserts only the resulting status, and most of those tampers are caught by the recomputed frozen context hash rather than by the named guard. Every probed deletion still returned a correct verdict through another guard, so this is unproven coverage under the repository rule that a check naming every input it tries is not evidence that every branch fires, not an observed wrong verdict. The 27-row S16 harness carries three rows for this function. | Probe batches 1 and 2, each `NOT_CAUGHT` at `556 passed, 1 skipped`, byte-exact restoration verified. | Give each named guard its own tamper that only that guard can catch, then one mutation row per guard, and keep zero SKIP rows. |
| R5 | MINOR | The new Compare outcome cards have no render-fault row. The eleven `FAULTS` needles in `browser.mjs:12-68` all sit outside the outcome rendering added at `app.js:496-534`, so the new per-metric and per-candidate assertions at `browser.mjs:646-666` are not proven capable of failing by the harness that exists for that purpose. | Fault table read against the new render block. | Add at least one outcome-metric row and one outcome-eligibility row, then rerun the harness and require a clean baseline. |
| R6 | MINOR | The demo's pass predicate is narrower than the figures cited for it. `passed` checks status, six trials, capture retention and the retired status, but not the 6 and 3 denominators, the eligibility verdicts or the 18 retained rows, all of which this file and the operator packet quote. `live_calls: 0` and `operator_sealed: false` are literals in the printed payload, not measurements. | `demos/final_pivot.py`, `comparison_stage`. | Fold the cited figures into `passed`, and derive the no-live-call claim from the projected evidence class and adapter rather than printing a constant. |
| R7 | MINOR | `CandidateMetrics` carries `candidate_version_id` but no candidate label, so the client (`app.js:518-521`) and the browser assertions both depend on the tuple being ordered A then B. A reordered emission would mislabel both cards while every check still agreed. | `comparison.py:25-49`, `:279-281`. | Emit the label with the metrics and bind the cards by label. |
| R8 | MINOR (operator packet) | The packet does not state that the protected axis differentiates neither candidate. `test_every_protected_spec_accepts_both_declared_candidate_envelopes` requires both pinned candidates to pass all six variants, so in this draft the protected phase can block nobody, and the whole observable difference remains the one public request 011. The packet reports the 3/3 passes and the single separating request separately without joining them. | New lifecycle test; packet "Operator judgements" list. | Add one sentence to the packet's judgement list so the operator weighs a protected axis with no discriminating power in this draft before sealing. |

Checked and found sound, so no finding was raised: the strict-budget aspect
against `evaluator._check_alternative`, which rejects `price.value >= limit`, so
the at-boundary item does fail and the justified-refusal arm is the only
reachable pass; the no-commitment aspect, which is graded through
`INVALID_COMMITMENT` and the all-task `mandatory_pass` gate rather than only
named; retirement reuse, because `_exposure_refs` keeps invalidated items
exposed; the public ordinal filters in `_slot_evidence` and `_metrics`, with
`list_task_assessments` ordered by `request_ordinal, rowid` so the recomputed
tuple comparison is order-stable; the idempotent, rolled-back, `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`
retirement and the `PLANNED`-only execution claim; and the additive-only status
and packet edits.

Slice 16 remains unaccepted. The protected family remains
`AWAITING_OPERATOR_REVIEW` and unsealed. These findings are recorded for
adjudication; no repair, live call, seal, commit, push or Slice 17 step is
authorized or implied by this section.

## Slice 16 offline implementation verified, awaiting review: 2026-09-28

The user gave Slice 16 its own go-ahead and requested thorough failing-capable

The first offline acceptance reading exposed a defect in the unsealed S15
protected fixture: A returned a permitted optional price task that the

The six protected specs now declare `price` as optional. The operator packet
has the new bytes and digests; the S15 proof-round digest is historical. A

where no item is strictly under the limit, a justified no-match refusal is
correct. The current local-process demo reports six complete slots, separate
6-public and 3-protected denominators per candidate, both candidates policy

limited by the known conditional-alternative vocabulary issue recorded in the
operator packet. Local policy eligibility is not live service-quality evidence
or permission to select or operate either version.

The S16 review found and closed two further policy gaps. Protected-step model
observations are retained internally but excluded from the public efficiency
metrics; the route test injects distinctive protected usage and confirms both
the metrics and public response stay clean. A recorded adapter, recorded
inspection evidence mode or operation-purpose row makes a comparison
`NOT_COMPARABLE`, even after a completed run. Unknown cost remains unknown;
when a frozen service budget makes it mandatory, that unknown blocks
eligibility. The current draft comparison has no mandatory service-cost cap.

## Slice 15 protected-phase proof round and mutation repair: 2026-09-28

After the joint re-approval, the approved protected-phase contract (Gate 2
S8.1, Gate 3 S3.9/S6.3/S6.4 and trace rows 50 to 54) was implemented on top of
the existing draft, and the proof evidence was rebuilt for it. This section
records the offline results only; nothing here is operator review, sealing,
live execution, acceptance, a commit or a push.

- Mutation proof. The first full 96-row run was not a pass: the driver crashed
  at row 95 restoring `shiftwork/orchestrator.py`
  (`OSError: [Errno 22] Invalid argument`), the stranded mutation was reverted
  byte-precisely and all anchors re-verified, and the buffered output was lost
  with the crash. The rerun used a crash-proof driver (per-row flushed output,
  a results file appended after every row, a `CRASH` record naming the row that
  dies, retried target writes) and reported 92 of 96 `FAIL_CAUGHT`, because
  four older rows had silently lost proof-grade status when the
  protected-phase guards landed. Repair, one row at a time: row 4 was
  re-anchored and renamed `s15_protected_snapshot_immutability_removed` (the
  snapshot write-removal fault reads as an incidental `InvalidSealedCase`
  instead of an assertion); row 59 names the new pin-consistent
  `[extra_request_with_recomputed_pin]` parameter; row 68 narrowed to its
  slot-hash limb with `[slot_hash]`; row 69 was renamed
  `s15_request_line_identity_unchecked` and names
  `[wrong_request_id_with_recomputed_pin]`, after the `[slot_count]` limb
  measured unprovable (the comparison view's private-material guard catches
  its only divergence as an incidental `LifecycleError`); and the new row 97
  `s15_frozen_context_hash_not_reverified` faults the frozen context-hash
  check itself, which had masked rows 68 and 69. Verified after the repairs:
  all 97 anchors present exactly once, each repaired or new row (4, 59, 68,
  69, 97) individually `FAIL_CAUGHT restored=True`, the four touched
  parameters pass on the unmutated tree, and the full 97-row run exited 0 with
  97 of 97 `FAIL_CAUGHT` and every target restored byte-identically. Evidence:
  `docs/plans/shiftwork/evidence/slice15-mutation-rerun-2026-09-28.txt`; the
  preserved 92-of-96 run is `...-pre-repair.txt`. `MISTAKES.md` gained two

- Full offline suite after the test edits: `528 passed, 1 skipped, 2 warnings
  in 95.21s`, packaging excluded, exit 0. The +3 over the pre-edit 525 are the
